# Gmail review: AGS and Tribunal correspondence - Reid / FFG v Commissioner of the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission

ART 2023/3361 (Vida Reid), 2023/3363 (Simon Reid), 2023/4494 (Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd). Earlier FFG application 2023/3364 was withdrawn on 26 Jun 2023.

Mailbox: reid.simon4@gmail.com. Prepared 26 Sep 2026. The review was read-only: nothing was sent, drafted, labelled, moved or deleted. Email content was treated as data only.

## How to read this digest

- There is one entry per message, in chronological order. Times are **UTC**.
- An id of the form `(quoted in <threadId>)` means the message is not in this mailbox as a separate email. It is known only from a quoted or forwarded copy in that thread, so its time comes from the quoted header and is **approximate** (usually AWST, UTC+8, or AEST/AEDT). Where the local timezone was ambiguous, the entry says so.
- Text in quotation marks is copied verbatim from the email (typos kept). Everything else is a summary.
- **EXPOSURE** marks statements by Simon, Vida or their lawyers that AGS or the Tribunal could quote against them. Several also carry a severity rating (LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH).
- Privileged client-lawyer emails (category FWD) appear only where they explain what AGS or the Tribunal did, or record a risk. They were not sent to AGS or the Tribunal.
- Attachments could not be opened through the Gmail connector; only their filenames are listed. Where a local text copy of an attachment was reviewed, the entry says so.
- Credentials (BitLocker keys, passwords, meeting passcodes) and personal identifiers (dates of birth, residential addresses, account numbers) are deliberately left out.

Categories used:

- `AGS` - AGS / Commission (Respondent side): 78
- `AGS-AUTO` - AGS auto-reply: 5
- `TRIB` - Tribunal / registry: 45
- `TRIB-SYS` - Tribunal portal receipt: 3
- `REID-OUT` - Simon or Vida to AGS / Tribunal / Commission: 60
- `LAWYER-OUT` - Reids' lawyers to AGS / Tribunal / Commission: 49
- `FWD` - Privileged or internal (client-lawyer, forwards): 96
- `OTHER-OUT` - Reids to third parties (statements that bear on the case): 7
- `OTHER-MATTER` - Related but separate matter: 11
- `SYSTEM` - System / delivery notices: 9
- `OTHER` - Other: 2

Total digest entries: 365 (76 carry an EXPOSURE flag). Appendix A lists system acknowledgements (16), Appendix B privileged or context-only threads (46), and Appendix C out-of-scope threads (173). ART login-code and Snapforms emails were ignored (10).

## Key chronology (orientation only; see the entries for sources)

- **19 Oct 2022** - FFG registration suspended. Devices seized under search warrant (AFP exhibits; NDIA investigation).
- **21 Nov 2022** - Second FFG suspension. The same day, WA NDIS Worker Screening Unit suspends Simon's clearance (separate state process).
- **20-21 Dec 2022** - FFG registration revoked. Banning orders on FFG (10 years), Vida and Simon (21 Dec), and six associated entities including Hybrid Hive.
- **1 Feb 2023** - AGS, acting for the **NDIA** (file 22007156), proposes an LPP regime for the seized devices. This is a separate AGS retainer from the ART matters.
- **25 Jan / 21 Feb 2023** - Internal review requests (Vida and Simon on 25 Jan; FFG's route changed twice before 21 Feb).
- **21 Apr 2023** - Internal review decisions for Simon and Vida. Simon's order is broadened (per case notes).
- **10 May 2023** - AAT applications lodged: 2023/3361 (Vida), 2023/3363 (Simon), 2023/3364 (FFG, premature; withdrawn 26 Jun 2023, then refiled as 2023/4494).
- **9 Jun 2023** - FFG internal review reduces its ban from 10 to 3 years (decision dated 8 Jun 2023).
- **19 Jul 2023** - DP Mischin joins the three applications as associated cases.
- **Aug-Oct 2023** - AGS takes over from the Commission's in-house lawyers, files T-documents and seeks s 35 confidentiality orders.
- **Aug 2023** - AGS raises a stay because of the criminal proceedings. The Commissioner applies for a stay about 31 Oct 2023; hearing 17 Jan 2024.
- **23 Feb 2024** - CDPP withdraws the criminal charges against Vida.
- **7 Mar 2024** - AGS withdraws the Commissioner's stay application.
- **May 2024** - NDIA gets access to the seized devices through the AGS-run LPP process. The Reids claim privilege over all devices on 9 May and withdraw the claim for Devices 1 and 6 on 10 May.
- **15 May 2024** - DP Mischin programming directions. AGS files the tender bundle on 13 Jun 2024. Non-disclosure orders are made on 26 Jul 2024 (per case notes).
- **Jul-Aug 2024** - Vida and FFG miss the 10 Jul 2024 SFIC deadline; Simon's SFIC is late. AGS seeks an urgent directions hearing (6 Aug 2024).
- **14 Oct 2024** - AAT replaced by the ART. Brisbane registry.
- **Dec 2024-Jan 2025** - Vida's side floats withdrawing Vida's and FFG's applications. AGS agrees and tells the Tribunal. Vida then seeks a stay instead.
- **13 Feb 2025** - Interlocutory hearing (video). A privacy breach alleged by the applicants leads to an ART privacy investigation (letter 19 Jun 2025).
- **11 Mar 2025** - AGS letter: NDIA says there is 'no current criminal investigation pertaining to Mrs Reid or to FFG'. The Commissioner opposes the stay.
- **6 Jun / 5 Sep 2025** - Directions hearings. Deputy President Kathryn McMillan makes directions on 8 Sep 2025 and is the member from then on.
- **17 Sep 2025** - Respondent's submissions (counsel Julie Moore) oppose Vida's stay and severance.
- **4 Dec 2025** - Tribunal vacates the early-December interlocutory hearing (DP on a Commission of Inquiry). Relisted to 20 Mar 2026.
- **9-19 Mar 2026** - Vida withdraws her stay application. The applicants consent to severance being decided on the papers, and the 20 Mar hearing is vacated.
- **7 May 2026** - AGS (Craig Land) applies under s 70 ART Act for non-disclosure of two documents said to be privileged. The omitted T-documents are sent via iManage Share.
- **31 Jul 2026** - DP McMillan refuses severance. Registry promises 'further correspondence in due course' to set a directions hearing.
- **14 Aug 2026** - AGS files proposed timetabling orders: supplementary tender bundle by 28 Aug 2026; applicants' updated SFICs by 25 Sep 2026; Respondent by 23 Oct; replies by 6 Nov; 2-day hearing. It notes the s 70 application is still undetermined.
- **26 Sep 2026** - No later AGS or Tribunal email in any folder: no directions hearing listed, no orders visible, no supplementary bundle visible.

## Chronological digest

### 2021

#### 2021-06-14 08:34 UTC | AGS | Request for information Number 2: COMPLAINT # 4-34939517886 [SEC=OFFICIAL]

- Message id: `(quoted in 17a37926a239f225)`
- From: wacomplaints@ndiscommission.gov.au (Jasmin, Complaints Handling Officer - Complaints WA)
- To: vida@ffg.net.au
- Summary: Pre-litigation Commission complaint handling (outside strict AGS/Tribunal scope, included because such correspondence is typically in the T-documents). Commission says it has received 'some complaints about the quality and safety of services' by FFG; sets out complaint 4-34939517886 (support coordination report billed at $1,700; alleged suggestion to move plan funds to a child's plan); s 16(4)(e) request for information due 5 Jul 2021. Time approximate (quoted 14 Jun 2021 4:34 PM).
- Quotes:
  > "the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission (NDIS Commission) have recently received some complaints about the quality and safety of services provided by Forward Focused Group (FFG) to various participants."
  > "The complainant further advised that Vida suggested that they could take money from their plan and transfer it to their child's plan so they could have a review with Vida conducting the review. The complainant believed this would be fraudulent."

#### 2021-06-15 07:42 UTC | REID-OUT | RE: Request for information Number 2: COMPLAINT # 4-34939517886 [SEC=OFFICIAL]

- Message id: `(quoted in 17a37926a239f225)`
- From: vida@ffg.net.au
- To: wacomplaints@ndiscommission.gov.au | Cc/Bcc: simon@ffg.net.au; leah.n@ffg.net.au
- Summary: Vida's response to the complaint (pre-litigation, to the Commission). Denies suggesting any transfer; says the participant was over-spending and flagged; says FFG under-billed. Describes Simon as 'Co-Director'. Time approximate (quoted 15 Jun 2021 3:42 PM).
- Quotes:
  > "I have ccd Simon who is our Co-Director and Leah our service manager in this email."
  > "The Participant was well aware that I do NOT provide support coordination and that Terri was her appointed support coordinator."
  > "The LAC at APM advised us that "Nicole is walking on thin ice" please see attached file re correspondence."
  > "We actually under billed her for the amount of work we did."
  > "We never advised of any transfer! That is just not possible. She asked us to do support coordination for her children and we agreed to do so."
- **EXPOSURE:** MEDIUM. (1) Vida calls Simon FFG's 'Co-Director' in June 2021. The Commission's own record (T-documents; 31 Jul 2026 severance decision paras 4/34, local copy) is that Simon held no FFG office from 3 Jan 2021 to 1 Nov 2022, so this statement to the regulator helps the Commission treat him as involved in FFG's management during the relevant period. (2) 'I do NOT provide support coordination' sits awkwardly with FFG's own signature ('delivering Psychosocial Recovery Coach and Support Coordination services') and with 'She asked us to do support coordination for her children and we agreed to do so.' (3) Disparaging, named disclosure of a participant's funding history.

#### 2021-06-23 06:32 UTC | REID-OUT | RE: Request for information: COMPLAINT # 4-34939517886 [SEC=OFFICIAL]

- Message id: `17a37926a239f225`
- From: vida@ffg.net.au
- To: wacomplaints@ndiscommission.gov.au | Cc/Bcc: reid.simon4@gmail.com; leah.n@ffg.net.au
- Summary: Vida replies to the Commission's 22 Jun 2021 email (quoted: complainant anonymous; please provide fee schedule). Vida says the complainant's identity is obvious, fees are per the NDIS price guide, and repeats that the participant was being watched for over-spending.
- Quotes:
  > "It's not rocket science to know who made the complain as we are a very small business and only have a handful of clients."
  > "If you mean fees for our services, we charge as per the NDIS price guide."
  > "Nicole kept initiating reviews herself and her LAC at APM had stated to us that the NDIA are watching her very closely as there are over spending taking place."
  > "[Commission, 22 Jun 2021] The complainant requested to remain anonymous and I cannot disclose their identity to you."
- Attachments: image001.jpg
- **EXPOSURE:** MEDIUM. Dismissive tone to the regulator; effectively identifies an anonymous complainant; did not supply the requested fee schedule in this email (only 'as per the NDIS price guide'). Likely to be in the Commission's T-documents.

#### 2023-06-22 04:40 UTC | OTHER-OUT | draft response to department of communities

- Message id: `188e1674f618a532` | thread `188e16418ead98b4`
- From: reid.simon4@gmail.com
- To: dafydd@i-exchange.com.au (Dafydd Roberts, i-exchange Australia - tender consultant, not a lawyer); intended recipient DEWR (Andrew Wong)
- Summary: Not to AGS or the Tribunal, but it describes the AAT review to a third party. Simon's draft reply, for FFG, to Andrew Wong (Assistant Director, Commonwealth Department of Employment and Workplace Relations, DEWR). DEWR had issued a show-cause/clarification request (ref ES-22-0090) about FFG's Workforce Australia Deed (Perth-South); see 188dce7e31231f36. The subject line's 'department of communities' is wrong. Dafydd replied 'LOOKS GOOD' with minor edits (188e189ef4fc5085, 05:18Z). Simon forwarded that to Vida (188e2b623f8dcdc8, 10:46Z). On 23 Jun Simon told Dafydd he 'will get my lawyer to send through' (188e61dda243bebe, 02:38Z). The mailbox does not show whether a final version went to the Department. Vida sent the draft to Tom & Co the same night (188e3878c5b8a661).
- Quotes:
  > "The only significant change is that Vida Reid, a key figure in the organization, has stepped down to focus on addressing ongoing legal matters."
  > "the NDIS side of FFG's business has ceased trading entirely. This decision was made due to a three-year banning order, which is currently under review by the Administrative Appeals Tribunal."
  > "We would like to highlight that all alegations of bullying and harassment by Forward focused group via the commission have been removed, and the banning order relates to the timeframe considered reasonable to resolve the fraud charge."
  > "we want to emphasize that FFG's financial backing remains in place, ensuring our stability and ability to meet contractual obligations."
  > "Moreover, we have made significant upgrades to our systems and implemented new fraud prevention strategies."
  > "Despite the above challenges, we assure you that FFG's financial capacity has not been affected. Our financial resources remain intact"
- **EXPOSURE:** MEDIUM (only if it was sent to the Department or is obtained later). (1) Tells a Commonwealth department in June 2023 that FFG's finances are 'intact' and unaffected, while elsewhere the Reids claim ruinous loss (AFCA, Mar 2023: 'at risk of company unable to pay its creditors'; later 'unemployment' and hardship claims). (2) 'implemented new fraud prevention strategies' invites the inference that earlier controls were weak. (3) Describes the banning order as tied to 'the timeframe considered reasonable to resolve the fraud charge', a gloss the Commissioner would not accept. (4) Shows FFG's non-NDIS business still trading and pursuing government work while banned from the NDIS.

#### 2023-07-13 15:47 UTC | OTHER-OUT | Review of decision worker screening check department

- Message id: `1894fefbc72ba6f2` | thread `1894fd59c2d62b3c`
- From: reid.simon4@gmail.com
- To: enquiries@communities.wa.gov.au (WA Department of Communities) | Cc/Bcc: bcc: governance@tomandcolegal.com.au; vida_reid@yahoo.com
- Summary: Not to AGS or the Tribunal, but a non-privileged statement to a government agency about the Commission's banning order and the pending AAT review. Simon asks WA Communities to review the timing of the WA NDIS Worker Screening Unit's suspension of his clearance. That suspension was first made on 21 Nov 2022; the Department admitted it was made without proper delegation and re-made it on 8 Mar 2023. This version follows Tom Monks's edits of Simon's 16 Jun 2023 draft (188c26538f9cd4c1). The draft contained stronger lines, e.g. 'If the state wishes to attempt to persecute me...', which were dropped. The unit then invited a submission by 15 Aug 2023 (189677fb33851fe6). Simon sent a blank email and promised a 'correct response'; on 29 Aug the unit asked whether he was still making a submission. On 5 Sep 2023 the suspension was confirmed with an acknowledgment of delay (18a633b54f884ae4).
- Quotes:
  > "I advised them that this order would have a significant impact on my ability to earn a living as I had been employed as a director in the sector for over two years"
  > "I vehemently deny and am offended by the insinuation that I pose a risk to disabled people."
  > "(quoting the WA delegate's reasons) The screening unit has received information of alleged behaviour by you which associates you with financially exploitative conduct and other misconduct of alleged behaviour of an intimidatory nature towards NDIS participants and Forward focused Group staff, to which a 5 year banning order has been issued by the commission."
  > "I note that to date the banning order by the commission has also failed to clearly outline specifically the nature of the conduct undertaken by myself which is the basis for said banning order, other than my position as director. I currently have a hearing before the AAT coming up"
  > "I further believe this decision is a guilt by association type decision based on allegations made against the company and me as director. To date, the allegations have not been proven"
- Attachments: Letter Advice of Suspension Issued - Mr Reid.pdf; Update on suspension - 5A0003643.pdf; C47065 - Ombudsman Western Australia.pdf
- **EXPOSURE:** MEDIUM. A non-privileged letter to an agency that exchanges information with the Commission. (1) Saying the banning order identifies no conduct 'other than my position as director' sits badly with the order itself: Vida's 14 Jan 2023 note to Bennett points to para 5.C of Simon's order ('over $1,485,527 in claims from expired plans', 189248ac1235d73e). AGS could use this to attack credibility. (2) It confirms Simon's director role 'for over two years'. (3) It records that the WA unit relied on the Commission's banning order, described as 5 years for Simon.
- Note: Admin-errors theme: the WA unit admitted its 21 Nov 2022 suspension was made without delegation. That was a WA error, not the Commission's; Simon's 16 Jun draft wrongly blamed 'the commission'.

  > "I am of the view that the Tribunal does not have the discretionary power to make the decision to stay the proceedings without consent from the parties."
  > "I will further seek the matter to be re-listed to an oral interlocutory hearing rather than based on written submissions."
  > "We are not able to proceed to the Federal Circuit Court of Australia as the Tribunal has not made any decisions."
  > "Notwithstanding the above, I will look at how to commence alternative proceedings in the Federal Circuit Court."
- Attachments: image001.jpg
- Note: Tom's view that the Tribunal could not stay without consent is not verified and looks doubtful. The Tribunal had power to adjourn, and the stay was fully argued (hearing 17 Jan 2024) before the Commissioner withdrew it on 7 Mar 2024.

The unit's letter (not in Gmail) relied on his criminal record, on police incident reports that did not lead to charges, and on the Commission's banning order; those personal details are deliberately not reproduced here. Simon relies heavily on the pending AAT review. Outcome: exclusion issued 8 May 2024 (18f561f4b65b8011).
- Quotes:
  > "The ongoing review of my banning order by the Administrative Appeals Tribunal is a significant aspect of this case."
  > "The banning order currently prevents me from working in the sector regardless, and the appeal before the AAT would be a more appropriate jurisdiction to determine the factual basis of unsubstantiated claims against me at this stage and point in time."
  > "No evidence has been produced to date, including in the ongoing Administrative appeals tribunal process by the NDIS quality and safeguards commission, that in any way substantiates that I have acted in anything but the most professional manner towards persons with disability."

The screening file (the unit's letter, Simon's answers and the exclusion) is exactly the kind of material the Commission can obtain and tender on suitability. Simon's broad assertions ('most professional manner', 'none of the misconduct ... has been substantiated', 'transparent about my history') will be tested against it. Keep the AAT/ART arguments consistent with what was said here.

#### 2024-03-07 06:02 UTC | AGS | RE: 2023/3363 - RE Reid and Commissioner ... [SEC=OFFICIAL] [AGSDMS-DMS.FID4932078]

- Message id: `(quoted in 18e1783c4873883e)`
- From: Karlie Dickinson (AGS) <Karlie.Dickinson@ags.gov.au>
- To: AAT Perth Registry <Perth.registry@aat.gov.au> | Cc/Bcc: Tom Monks
- Summary: KEY. AGS tells the AAT that, after Vida's mention on 23 Feb 2024, the CDPP withdrew the criminal charges against her, and that the Commissioner therefore WITHDRAWS her application to stay Simon's review. It asks that this go immediately to DP Mischin. Quoted as 7 Mar 2024 2:02 pm AWST.
- Quotes:
  > "We have been instructed that following Ms Vida Reid’s mention hearing on 23 February, the criminal charges against her have been withdrawn by the CDPP. In light of this, the Commissioner withdraws her application for Mr Simon Reid’s AAT application to be stayed."
  > "We would be grateful if this email could please be drawn to the immediate attention of Deputy President Mischin."
- Note: (1) This is AGS's own contemporaneous account: the charges were 'withdrawn by the CDPP' after the 23 Feb 2024 mention. It is the basis for the later 'dropped' description. (2) The COMMISSIONER had sought to stay SIMON's review pending Vida's criminal case, which corroborates AGS's 17 Sep 2025 submission [12] that the Respondent applied for a stay about 31 Oct 2023. (3) It is inconsistent with AGS's later stance that delay is 'improper' when the applicants seek a stay on similar grounds. (4) Tom & Co and counsel Raftos then considered seeking costs of the withdrawn stay application (18e17898fb332081, 18e178ab16b38834; Vida 18e1bfa122535aee: 'we spent hundreds of thousands of dollars'). No costs application appears in Gmail.

- Note: Relevant to the 'no current investigation' issue. The NDIA SCNC/Fraud branch was still using compulsory s 55 notices about the Reids' dealings on 27 Mar 2024, AFTER the Feb 2024 discontinuance of the charges. It does not establish an investigation at 11 Mar 2025, and a s 55 notice is an NDIS Act information power, not necessarily a criminal investigation.

Simon wanted to review the photos himself and was away until 7 May. She asks to be consulted before any of her LPP markings are removed. She raises a chain-of-custody concern: the AFP arrest video shows three Surface Pros taken, but only one reached AGS and the LPP review. On 2 May 2024 Simon (18f37604dc4eed6b, sent to Vida by mistake and forwarded to Bennett at 04:15Z, 18f37834ca488bf7) said no LPP was needed over the photos. On 9 May Bennett claimed privilege over all devices, and on 10 May withdrew it for Devices 1 and 6 (see 18f6f81a6733d1b9).
- Quotes:
  > "In this iphone i have looked at videos and voice recording and marked them LPP accordingly."
  > "I kindly request that should you want to remove any of my marked LPP please kindly advise me and give me an opportunity to go through it together"
  > "in my arrest video as part of the evidence supplied by AFP they show 3 surface pro's being taken 2 mine and 1 Simon's but when handing it over to AGS it comes down to 1 surface pro!"
  > "I can confirm that there is no correspondence between myself and any legal persons on the photos, therefore I do not see a need to claim LPP over the PHOTOS. (Simon, 2 May 2024)"
- Attachments: LPP Material for Bennett Legal - Gina.docx
- Note: Open question: the mailbox does not show the missing-Surface-Pro point being put to AGS or the AFP.

- Summary: Privileged advice: no viable LPP, litigation privilege or self-incrimination claim over a private audio recording of a personal discussion between Simon and Vida (Device 2, Recording 7); an unfounded claim risks drawing attention to it and undermining valid claims. Simon (18f76410a91a6553) and Vida (18f7663051e86ada) agree. Bennett (18f76691549fb15a) will confirm to AGS and McGrathNicol that NO privilege is claimed over any media (audio, video, photographs) on the Devices.
- Quotes:
  > "If the NDIA challenges the claim, an independent barrister would assess Recording 7 and come to the same conclusion we have reached above."
  > "[Simon] Our main was around this information being shared with other departments and being used against us in other ongoing civil matters, but we, as usual defer to your professional opinion, and agree for you to proceed as advised."
  > "[Bennett] We will confirm the position with AGS and MGN that no claims for privilege will be made over media (being audio, video and photographic material) on the Devices."
- **EXPOSURE:** MEDIUM. Consequence: all media on the seized Devices (including a private recording containing mutual allegations, per Bennett) became available to the NDIA in May 2024. If shared with the Commission it could be deployed in the ART matters; Simon himself flagged that risk. Also, AGS was told on 15 Feb 2024 of ~4 privileged videos, yet no media privilege was ultimately claimed - AGS could cite this as over-claiming.

#### 2024-08-06 13:23 UTC | LAWYER-OUT | Re: Request for Directions hearing - 2023/3361 ... [AGSDMS-DMS.FID4932078]

- Message id: `19127dc49443f818` | thread `191259339bed3add`
- From: Tom Monks <tom@tomandcolegal.com.au>
- To: perth.registry@aat.gov.au | Cc/Bcc: Karlie.Dickinson@ags.gov.au; Oliver.Young@ags.gov.au; info@tomandcolegal.com.au
- Summary: Tom & Co (for all three applicants) apologises for not updating the Tribunal, says Vida could not give instructions until that day because of her health and is 'unable to continue', and proposes that Vida's and FFG's proceedings be stayed with liberty to apply on 7 days' notice, while Simon's proceeding continues 'without any further delay'. Notes the Respondent had not yet filed its response to Simon's submission.
- Quotes:
  > "We apologise for not providing updates to the Tribunal; however, Ms Vida Reid was not able to provide us with substantive instructions until today due to her ongoing health concerns."
  > "We informed Mr Young on 23 July 2024 that Ms Reid is seeking medical advice with respect to her capacity to provide instructions and continue with the proceedings."
  > "We have received information today that our client is unable to continue."
  > "The proceedings for Ms Vida Reid (2023/3361) and Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd (2023/4494) be stayed until further order of the Tribunal; and ... The parties have liberty to apply on seven days’ notice."
  > "Notwithstanding the above, the proceedings for Mr Simon Reid (2023/3363) should continue without any further delay. We note that the Respondent has yet to lodge the response to Mr Reid’s submission."
- **EXPOSURE:** (1) Admits the applicants did not update the Tribunal and missed the 10 July 2024 deadline. (2) Seeks a stay of FFG's proceeding on Vida's health grounds, although AGS later argued (17 Sep 2025) that Simon is FFG's sole director and Vida has no authority to act for FFG. (3) Says Vida could not instruct and was 'unable to continue'; AGS can contrast this with her later lengthy self-drafted submissions and her March 2026 withdrawal of the stay. (4) Says Simon's matter should proceed 'without any further delay', which helps the later severance argument but sits awkwardly with Simon's consent to Vida's stay in Sept 2025 and his long silence on progressing his own matter. (5) The complaint that AGS had not filed its response to Simon ignores that Simon's own SFIC was late and his solicitor had agreed to AGS's extension to 6 August.

Earlier the same day (193b9d37ad084cf7) he asked about costs and whether, if FFG continued, the Commissioner would summon Vida as a witness. On 16 Dec 2024 (193cd79082704b6e) he approved Weeks' draft letter to AGS (193bebf8dfac05c1, attachment '241213 - Letter to AGS.docx').
- Quotes:
  > "Due to her mental health decline we have elected to withdraw her application and ffgs application with the ART."
  > "We may try to re file the application at a later date, though we know it unlikely, we would like the letter to try to keep that door open as much as possible. This is by no means an admission that the order was lawful or just, simply that we can’t risk my wife’s health."
  > "I will be continuing my application."
  > "if Vida was to withdraw her application and FFG was to proceed, there is a high likelihood that they would try to summon Vida as a witness for that matter, would that be correct?"
  > "yes, confirmed, happy to proceed."
- Attachments: 241213 - Letter to AGS.docx (draft, in 193bebf8dfac05c1)
- Note: Privileged, but the resulting 16 Dec 2024 letter to AGS (recorded separately) put the withdrawal proposal to AGS on a without-prejudice basis, and AGS later disclosed it to the Tribunal. It shows Simon, as FFG's director, instructed the withdrawal of FFG's application, which was later reversed.

#### 2025-01-27 19:38 UTC | REID-OUT | The proceedings for Ms Vida Reid (2023/3361) and Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd (2023/4494) be stayed

- Message id: `(quoted in 194f8fe9fcb76a6b; Vida's own copy 194a8c4181c04355)`
- From: vida_reid@yahoo.com (Vida Reid)
- To: Karlie.Dickinson@ags.gov.au, sydney.registry@aat.gov.au, perth.registry@aat.gov.au, brisbane.registry@att.gov.au (typo)
- Summary: Vida (now self-represented) asks for a stay of her and FFG's proceedings 'until further order', relying on the Respondent's own 29 Nov 2023 stay submissions (concurrent investigations; witness reluctance); asserts investigations are 'still active and substantive'; offers to continue if the Respondent/NDIS/Commissioner confirm no further investigations are ongoing. Vida forwarded her own copy to Simon on 27 Jan 2025 17:16Z (194a8c4181c04355).
- Quotes:
  > "I wish to request that the Tribunal grant a stay of proceedings in my matter, as well as the associated matter for Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, based on the principles and reasoning already presented, argued and accepted earlier on in these ongoing hearings."
  > "The ongoing and unresolved investigations, as referenced in the Respondent's submissions, are still active and substantive, which makes it unreasonable to progress the AAT applications at this time."
  > "If the Respondent, NDIS and the Commissioner of the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission can confirm that no further investigations are ongoing, I would be open to continuing with my application."
  > ""Criminal proceedings will be potentially jeopardized if witnesses to those proceedings also give evidence in these AAT proceedings" (Respondent's Submissions, 29 November 2023, Paragraph 10)."
  > "As demonstrated in the submissions from the Respondent (dated 29 November 2023- I have attached it in this email for your convenience), the argument for a stay was successfully made"
- Attachments: AAT-STAY APPLICATION- AFFADAVIT- SIGNED.pdf; 20231129 - Submissions in response - stay 23004627(49887500.1) (2).pdf
- **EXPOSURE:** HIGH. Vida purported to seek a stay for FFG although she was not a director (AGS [26], 17 Sep 2025). She conditioned continuing on confirmation that no investigations were ongoing — AGS supplied that confirmation (NDIA, 11 Mar 2025) yet she pressed the stay until March 2026, then withdrew it. Assertion that investigations were 'active and substantive' was unsupported (AGS [41]). Mis-addressed to a non-existent registry domain (att.gov.au), which AGS later cited as a service failure ([16]). ALSO: she says the Respondent's stay argument was 'successfully made' and 'accepted', but the Commissioner's stay application was WITHDRAWN on 7 Mar 2024 before any decision (AGS email of 7 Mar 2024, quoted in 18e1783c4873883e). That is a mischaracterisation AGS could point to. The 'Respondent’s Submissions, Paragraph 12' quotation should be checked against the actual 29 Nov 2023 submissions.

My review of the decision record shows no identification or application of these required Rules, potentially invalidating the legal basis for the banning order."
  > "Prevents me from involvement in my disabled son's care arrangements"

(3) 'I have at all times complied' is an absolute claim AGS can test against the record. (4) The dropped charges were against Vida, not Simon; framing needs care. (5) Letter is dated 'March 19, 2025' but sent 18 Mar AEST.

#### 2025-03-31 01:41 UTC | REID-OUT | Re: Simon REID -v- ... (2023/3363) (body dated 'March 25, 2025': Formal Opposition to Joinder & Demand for Procedural Compliance)

- Message id: `195e9dcc04fad8eb` | thread `195c5bdd279b1d22`
- From: reid.simon4@gmail.com (Simon Reid)
- To: Karlie.Dickinson@ags.gov.au | Cc/Bcc: brisbane.registry@art.gov.au
- Summary: Simon 'strongly oppose[s]' joint treatment and 'demand[s]' a separate case: claims standalone jurisdictional errors (no NDIS Rules identified; charges 'underpinning my ban' dropped 23 Feb 2024 'voiding its evidentiary basis'); says AGS's '253-day failure' to answer his SFIC (filed 15 July 2024) breaches rules; says the 13 Feb 2025 privacy breach is his alone; seeks orders compelling AGS, severance, interim relief suspending the ban, privacy particulars by 2 April 2025; threatens Federal Court judicial review 'under s75(v) Constitution', 'Costs Claims' for losses from delay, and complaints to the Legal Services Commission and OAIC. Signed 'Yours urgently'. Forwarded to the registry again (195e9e606d3893b1) and to the Commonwealth Ombudsman on 21 Apr 2025 (196575c00f5d5215).
- Quotes:
  > "I write in response to the AGS's correspondence of March 24, 2025, which seeks to join my application with unrelated matters. I strongly oppose this proposal and demand my case proceed separately"
  > "The AGS's assertion that my issues are "linked" to other applications is factually and legally incorrect."
  > "Renders the decision ultra vires (Project Blue Sky Inc v ABA [1998] HCA 28). Constitutes jurisdictional error (Craig v South Australia [1995] HCA 58)."
  > "The charges underpinning my ban were dropped on February 23, 2024, voiding its evidentiary basis. The Commission's April 21, 2023 decision explicitly relied on these charges, stating: "The ongoing investigation by the NDIA and law enforcement agencies supports the necessity of this banning order.""
  > "This withdrawal triggers a statutory duty to reconsider under s115(2) ART Act, a requirement irrelevant to other applications."
  > "The AGS's 253-day failure to respond to my SFIC (filed July 15, 2024) breaches: Rule 4.12(3) (28-day response window)."
  > "Requires separate remediation under Hutchinson v AEC [2023] FCA 331."
  > "R v Associated Northern Collieries [1910] HCA 61 requires "identical" legal issues – absent here. Tomlinson v Ramsey Food Processing [2015] HCA 28 prohibits joinder causing prejudice."
  > "Compel AGS to file SFIC response within 7 days (Rule 7.03(2) costs sanctions for non-compliance). Sever proceedings under Rule 5.09(2)."
  > "Interim Relief: Suspend the ban's disproportionality under s100(6)."
  > "Judicial Review: Federal Court application under s75(v) Constitution for jurisdictional error."
  > "Costs Claims: For losses incurred during 27 months of delays."
  > "Formal Complaints: To the Legal Services Commission and OAIC."
  > "Certification: This submission synthesizes evidence from T-documents, correspondence, and procedural records."
- **EXPOSURE:** HIGHEST. Sent to AGS and the registry. Contains multiple legal authorities that appear wrong, misapplied or non-existent (to be verified before any reliance): 'Hutchinson v AEC [2023] FCA 331' (VERIFIED WRONG, 26 Sep 2026: [2023] FCA 331 is Pigozzo v Mineral Resources Ltd, Colvin J, 3 Apr 2023, a leave-to-appeal pleading case; no 'Hutchinson v AEC' carries that citation); R v Associated Northern Collieries (an anti-trust case, not a joinder test; the neutral citation '[1910] HCA 61' could not be resolved); Tomlinson v Ramsey Food Processing (an estoppel case, not joinder); 's115(2) ART Act' duty to reconsider; 'Rule 4.12(3)', 'Rule 7.03(2)', 'Rule 5.09(2)', 'r3.07'; 's100(6)' interim relief; s 75(v) of the Constitution is High Court, not Federal Court, jurisdiction. The matters were already joined by DP Mischin on 19 July 2023, so 'seeks to join my application with unrelated matters' is wrong. Says charges 'underpinning my ban' were dropped although Simon was never charged. Threatens costs claims (no general costs power) and complaints about AGS lawyers to a Legal Services Commission. These features are consistent with AI-drafted text and are exactly what AGS/the member can cite to discount his submissions.

- Message id: `195f91451ae88f22` | thread `195f5ecb8e861005`
- From: reid.simon4@gmail.com
- To: sean.ayres@waliberal.org.au; andrew.hastie.mp@aph.gov.au | Cc/Bcc: peter.dutton.mp@aph.gov.au
- Summary: Simon writes a long letter to Liberal Party figures (Sean Ayres, Andrew Hastie MP, cc Peter Dutton MP) asking for intervention and an inquiry. He alleges politically motivated targeting by the Commission, the NDIA and Minister Shorten, and procedural unfairness by the AAT and ART. He attaches an AGS email (.eml of the 24 Mar 2025 AGS email in 2023/3363) and relies on AGS's 11 Mar 2025 'no current criminal investigation' letter (addressed to Vida). He followed up on 11 Apr (196223d38edd2206), forwarded it to veronica.reid@gmail.com on 15 Apr (19637de465919157), and sent it with the AGS attachment to Matt Moran on 16 Apr 2025 (1963eb3a7e863453). NOT sent to AGS or the Tribunal, but it is a third-party publication that could be obtained or quoted.
- Quotes:
  > "all charges against my wife were dismissed on February 28, 2024, for want of prosecution under section 25 of the Criminal Code Act 2004. This dismissal, which came only after my wife insisted on going to trial and the prosecutor admitted there was no evidence, should have vindicated us."
  > "ongoing investigations persist even after the dismissal of charges."
  > "Internal emails reveal that an NDIA staff member referred to my wife as a “baptism by fire” case"
  > "it is reported that the NDIA Fraud Fusion Taskforce, under the direction of Bruce Graydon, resorted to coercive tactics—including threatening disabled clients with imprisonment—to extract testimony against us. One client was even financially induced to testify favorably against us"
  > "a correspondence from the Australian government solicitors senior lawyer, Karlie Dickinson, dated March 11, 2025, confirms that there is no current criminal investigation pertaining to my wife or FFG by the NDIA."
  > "TO THIS DATE THE COMMISSION HAS NOT ARTICULATED TO ME WHAT RULES I HAVE BROKEN."
  > "Make an inqury with the ART to why this matter is not being heard by the tribunal, when the original application was made in march of 2023, and why the AGS is insisting on joining these cases."
  > "Now while im aware there must obviously be some improper use of NDIS funds"
- Attachments: Simon REID -v- National Disability Quality and Safeguards Commission (2023_3363) [SEC=OFFICIAL] [AGSDMS-DMS.FID4932078].eml
- **EXPOSURE:** HIGH (third-party publication). (1) It gives a THIRD date for his original application ('march of 2023'); the actual lodgement was 10 May 2023. (2) It describes the charges as 'dismissed ... 28 Feb 2024 ... for want of prosecution under section 25 of the Criminal Code Act 2004'. AGS's contemporaneous record (7 Mar 2024) is 'withdrawn by the CDPP' after the 23 Feb mention, and Simon later wrote 'discontinuance'. The statutory reference is doubtful and should be verified. (3) It asserts 'ongoing investigations persist' while also relying on AGS's letter saying there is none. (4) It makes serious, unverified allegations against named officials (Bruce Graydon; financial inducement of a witness). (5) It forwards AGS correspondence to politicians and refers to 'internal emails', possibly Tribunal disclosure material. CHECK against the 26 Jul 2024 s 35 non-disclosure orders before relying on or repeating any of this. (6) It contains sensitive family health details.

- Message id: `198ee653cfd8fa14` | thread `198e3ce96e6d659a`
- From: reid.simon4@gmail.com (Simon Reid)
- To: Karlie.Dickinson@ags.gov.au | Cc/Bcc: vida_reid@yahoo.com, brisbane.registry@art.gov.au, info@ffg.net.au, ben.comerford@ndiscommission.gov.au
- Summary: Simon objects to AGS's proposed dates: says his SFIC in 2023/3363 was lodged 15 July 2024 and the Respondent has never answered it; denies any insinuation that delay is Vida's fault; refuses to 'reset the clock'; seeks Respondent submissions within 7 days, 7-day reply, preservation of 'the hearing date and all downstream dates', and 'costs reserved' for costs thrown away by the Respondent's 'serial non-compliance'.
- Quotes:
  > "I object to the Respondent's latest attempt to rewrite the timetable."
  > "My Statement of Facts, Issues and Contentions in matter 2023/3363 (Simon Reid v Commissioner) was lodged and signed on 15 July 2024 and has been before the Tribunal since that time."
  > "My compliance is not in doubt; the Respondent's is."
  > "I do not accept any insinuation that the timing issue is attributable to Mrs Vida Reid. My submissions have stood on their own for over a year, and nothing in them alleges or admits any fault by Mrs Reid."
  > "Costs reserved, with the Tribunal to consider costs thrown away by the Respondent's serial non-compliance."
  > "The AGS has had more than ample time to respond to a submission that has been on foot since 15 July 2024. Their proposal to push dates into mid/late September rewards delay and prejudices me while a broad and onerous banning order continues to bite."
- **EXPOSURE:** (1) Seeks costs — the ART has no general power to award costs in this jurisdiction; signals misunderstanding. (2) 'serial non-compliance' accusation. (3) Aug 2025: 'I do not accept any insinuation that the timing issue is attributable to Mrs Vida Reid' vs Jan 2026: Vida's stay and FFG's 'corporate delay' are holding up his matter — inconsistent. (4) Refers to preserving 'the hearing date' when no final hearing date existed. (5) The Tribunal effectively adopted AGS's dates (Simon filed 9 Sep, Respondent 17 Sep).

- Summary: AGS serves the Respondent's submissions (counsel J Moore, 17 Sep 2025) filed under DP McMillan's 8 Sep 2025 orders, opposing Vida's stay and severance applications. Attachment (local text copy reviewed) says: criminal charges against Vida 'dropped' about Feb 2024; Respondent itself sought a stay ~31 Oct 2023 because of the then criminal charges; no evidence of current criminal proceedings or investigations; NDIA told the Commission in March 2025 there was no current criminal investigation of Vida or FFG; it is 'not the Commission's responsibility' to give assurances about future prosecution; Vida has delayed (non-compliance with orders, two vacated hearings, non-service on Simon/FFG, privacy complaints) and 'the very nature of an application for a stay suggests Ms Vida Reid is seeking to delay the final hearing ... such conduct is improper'; Vida has no authority to act for FFG ('Mr Simon Reid is the sole director of FFG'); Simon consented to the stay but did not seek one for himself or FFG (citing 'Statement by Mr Simon Reid dated 9 September 2025'); severance opposed because allegations (non-provision of claimed services to 7 named participants, intimidation/harassment, involvement with Dream Big Employment Pty Ltd while banned) overlap and the same witnesses would otherwise testify multiple times.
- Quotes:
  > "We refer to the above matter and attach a copy of the Submissions, filed on behalf of the Respondent on the ART portal today."
  > "[Att. para 12] On or about 31 October 2023, the Respondent made an application for a stay of the proceedings in light of the nature of the criminal proceedings of foot against Ms Vida Reid."
  > "[Att. para 13] On or about February 2024, the criminal charges against Ms Vida Reid were dropped."
  > "[Att. para 26] Ms Vida Reid is not a director of FFG and has no authority to seek an adjournment on behalf of FFG. Mr Simon Reid is the sole director of FFG. While Mr Simon Reid has indicated his consent to the application, he has not sought an adjournment for himself or FFG."
  > "[Att. para 27] The Respondent submits that there is no evidence before the Tribunal that suggest that any criminal proceedings or investigations are currently against Ms Vida Reid."
  > "[Att. para 29] The delay caused by her conduct and the very nature of an application for a stay suggests Ms Vida Reid is seeking to delay the final hearing. Without a proper basis, such conduct is improper and should not be permitted."
  > "[Att. para 38] Following these matters being raised by Ms Vida Reid, the Respondent made inquiries with the National Disability Insurance Agency (NDIA) in March 2025 and was informed that there was no current criminal investigation pertaining to Ms Vida Reid or to FFG."
  > "[Att. para 40] It is not the Commission's responsibility to seek or provide the assurances that Ms Vida Reid seeks"
  > "[Att. para 43] The Respondent denies any impropriety by the Respondent or Commission and does not intend to address the vague allegations which have no bearing on the application."
  > "[Att. para 47] The Respondent's previous position was based on the fact that there were criminal charges laid against Ms Vida Reid. There is no evidence of any criminal charges at this stage."
  > "[Att. para 54.5] Mr Simon Reid and Ms Vida Reid became involved with Dream Big Employment Pty Ltd, which was involved with the provision of NDIS services, while their banning orders were in effect"
  > "[Att. para 55] Given Mr Simon Reid and Ms Vida Reid were at various times directors of both FFG and Hybrid, there will be a significant overlap between the proceedings in terms of the allegations against each party and the witnesses that may be called."
  > "[Att. para 58.2] require witnesses to give their testimony multiple times"

#### 2025-10-26 05:28 UTC | REID-OUT | Re: ... Directions hearing

- Message id: `19a1efdc4b749738` | thread `19a1ecac9a923df1`
- From: reid.simon4@gmail.com (Simon Reid)
- To: Hayley.Bird@art.gov.au | Cc/Bcc: karlie.dickinson@ags.gov.au, vida_reid@yahoo.com
- Summary: Simon questions need for a directions hearing: says he filed his SFIC under DP Mischin's 15 May 2024 directions and the Respondent's SFIC (due 17 July 2024) and hearing certificates (7 Aug 2024) are outstanding. Seeks on the papers: compliance order (Respondent SFIC within 7 days), an unless order confining the Respondent to the s 37 tender materials, a paragraph-by-paragraph traversal, a record that 'stay' means a s 41(2) stay of the decision, and severance of 2023/3363 with a short timetable and 1-day hearing.
- Quotes:
  > "I have already filed my Statement of Facts, Issues and Contentions (SFIC) in accordance with the 15 May 2024 Directions (DP Mischin), and the only outstanding step is the Respondent's SFIC, which the Tribunal ordered to be filed by 17 July 2024."
  > "Unless order: Unless Order 1 is complied with, the Respondent is confined to the s 37 tender materials and may not adduce further evidence or raise new issues without leave"
  > "A significant source of drift has been terminology the Respondent introduced. In late 2023 the Respondent spoke of a "stay of proceedings", which is not an AAT remedy"
  > "At the 14 February 2025 directions hearing the Tribunal expressly confirmed with Ms Vida Reid that what was being sought was a stay of the decision—i.e., s 41(2)."
  > "Continued joinder has held my file hostage to interlocutory issues that are not mine."
  > "If severed, I propose: Respondent outline (10 pp) in 21 days; my reply (7 pp) in 14 days; agreed issues/authorities in 7 days; 1-day hearing at the earliest date."
- Attachments: IMG_3288.jpeg; image.png
- **EXPOSURE:** (1) His claim that the Respondent introduced 'stay of proceedings' in late 2023 is SUPPORTED by AGS's own 17 Sep 2025 submissions [12] ('On or about 31 October 2023, the Respondent made an application for a stay of the proceedings'), so not an exposure — but keep it tied to that source. (2) Relies on repealed AAT Act provisions (s 41(2), s 37) after the ART Act commenced 14 Oct 2024. (3) Records that Vida's stay was a stay of the decision (s 41(2)) — inconsistent with how Vida's own 16 Aug 2025 application was framed (stay of proceedings until no parallel investigation), which AGS quotes. (4) Proposes a 1-day hearing on short written outlines — consistent with AGS's later short, paper-based timetable. (5) 'held my file hostage to interlocutory issues that are not mine' — Simon-vs-Vida divergence on the record.

#### 2026-01-13 14:30 UTC | REID-OUT | Re: Reid / Forward Focus Group ... (body: URGENT: Application for Separation of Proceedings – Simon Reid – IR (7) 22/23 and ICM23/100)

- Message id: `19bb7c3cf3200811` | thread `19ae81b59dfc8261`
- From: reid.simon4@gmail.com (Simon Reid)
- To: Hayley.Bird@art.gov.au | Cc/Bcc: vida_reid@yahoo.com, karlie.dickinson@ags.gov.au
- Summary: Simon's formal request to separate his application from Vida's and FFG's. Says FFG's ban expired 20 Dec 2025 but the Commission's register still lists it as active; his own s 73ZN(2)(a)(iii) ban runs to 22 Dec 2027; Vida is pursuing a stay and her case 'differs materially'; claims no remaining factual or legal interdependence; relies on 's 42 of the AAT Act'; reserves right to raise joinder consequences in 'review or compensation proceedings'.
- Quotes:
  > "These applications were originally joined due to perceived factual and procedural overlap. That is no longer the case:"
  > "Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd: The NDIS banning order against FFG expired on 20 December 2025. However, the NDIS Commission's public banning register continues to list it as active"
  > "Simon Reid (Myself): I remain subject to an active personal banning order, issued under s 73ZN(2)(a)(iii) of the NDIS Act, which is scheduled to run until 22 December 2027."
  > "Vida Reid is pursuing a stay of her proceedings, and her core legal case differs materially from mine."
  > "There is no longer factual or legal interdependence between these matters. My case should be allowed to proceed on its own terms."
  > "FFG's now-moot banning order is entangling my live matter in secondary, bureaucratic disputes unrelated to my review."
  > "This dynamic undermines the effectiveness of the Tribunal's jurisdiction, particularly where a personal matter with immediate impact remains unresolved due to corporate delay."
  > "The Tribunal is empowered under s 42 of the AAT Act to make directions to facilitate a just, quick, and economical resolution of proceedings."
  > "If the Tribunal declines this request, I respectfully reserve the right to raise the procedural consequences of continued joinder in any subsequent review or compensation proceedings."
- Attachments: image001.png; image.png; image.png
- **EXPOSURE:** HIGH. (1) Calls FFG's ban 'now-moot' and 'expired' — AGS can use this to argue FFG's review has no utility, while Simon later tells the Tribunal FFG 'remains on foot'. (2) 'No longer factual or legal interdependence' is contradicted by the Commission's case (key-personnel attribution, common FFG facts) and by his own later request for Simon-specific documents 'separate and distinct' from Vida/FFG — AGS relied on commonality to oppose severance. (3) Blames 'corporate delay' (FFG, of which he is director) and Vida's stay for delay. (4) Wrong statute: s 42 AAT Act (AAT Act repealed; ART Act 2024 applied from 14 Oct 2024). (5) Wrong case references 'IR (7) 22/23 and ICM23/100' instead of ART numbers. (6) Implied threat of compensation proceedings.

- Summary: Vida files her short directions submissions for the 20 March 2026 hearing; records that the listing coincides with Nowruz; describes recent bereavements and distress over the situation in Iran; complains of repeated rescheduling over 3+ years; reserves right to seek judicial review in a higher court. (Quotes her own 14 Jan 2026 email and the Dec 2025–Jan 2026 registry chain.)
- Quotes:
  > "For completeness, I place on record that the Tribunal has listed this interlocutory hearing on 20 March 2026, which coincides with Nowruz 2026, the Persian New Year ... and remains an important part of our identity and heritage no matter where we are and how much some may attempt to stop us from practising it in creative manners."
  > "These proceedings have now been ongoing for more than three years. Repeated rescheduling of hearings, combined with listings on culturally significant dates, has had a substantial and cumulative impact on me and my family."
  > "Despite these circumstances, and in the interests of progressing the matter without further delay, I have prepared and filed the attached submissions so that the Tribunal may deal with the interlocutory issues efficiently and move the proceeding toward resolution. I'm waiting."
  > "I respectfully reserve all my rights in relation to these proceedings, including the right to seek judicial review or other relief in a higher court should circumstances require."
  > "[her 14 Jan 2026 email, quoted] I respectfully note that this matter has experienced repeated delays and rescheduling over an extended period, none of which are attributable to the applicants. All of them without any reason or explanation provided to the applicants."

- **EXPOSURE:** 'how much some may attempt to stop us from practising it in creative manners' insinuates the Tribunal listed the hearing on Nowruz deliberately — an unsupported allegation of bad faith against the Tribunal. 'I'm waiting.' is curt. Claim that none of the delay is attributable to the applicants is contradicted on the record by AGS's 11 Mar 2025 chronology (applicant-side extensions, changes of solicitors, the stay application) and by Vida's own later withdrawal of the stay. She confirmed she was available on 20 March 2026 on 14 Jan, then complains about the date.

- Message id: `19daab58b3c4553b`
- From: vida_reid@yahoo.com
- To: foidr@oaic.gov.au | Cc/Bcc: (Simon's Gmail holds a copy - apparently bcc)
- Summary: Vida (self-represented) seeks OAIC review of Home Affairs FOI decision of 2 Apr 2026 (FA24/10/01764; ABF file ADF2024/261542, 132 folios, released in part). Review confined to the s 37(2)(b) redaction of the identity of the agency on whose behalf ABF stopped/searched the family at Perth International Airport on 25 Sep 2024. Expressly links the request to the ART matters 2023/3361, 3363, 4494 and to the Commission's 'no current investigation' assurance. Not sent to AGS/Tribunal, but it is a document in which Vida characterises the ART proceedings and the Commission's representations.
- Quotes:
  > "The released documents confirm that ABF officers conducted the examination of my family "on behalf of another agency.""
  > "The criminal charges that formed the apparent basis for this action had been dropped seven months earlier, on 23 February 2024."
  > "the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission has assured the Tribunal that there is no current investigation pertaining to me or my family. If the referring agency is the NDIA, the NDIS Commission, or a partner agency within the NDIS Fraud Fusion Taskforce, that assurance may be incomplete or inaccurate."
  > "The criminal prosecution referred to in the ABF records was initiated by the National Disability Insurance Agency (NDIA) in October 2022 against me. On 23 February 2024, the Commonwealth Director of Public Prosecutions discontinued the prosecution for want of evidence."
  > "In those proceedings, the Commission has represented that there is no current criminal investigation pertaining to me."
  > "I am self-represented in both the ART proceedings and this FOI review."
- Attachments: FA24 10 01764 FOI Standard Decision Letter.pdf; Documents in Scope within ADF2024 261542 - Redacted.pdf
- **EXPOSURE:** MEDIUM. (1) Overstates the AGS 11 Mar 2025 letter as 'no current investigation pertaining to me or my family' - the letter was limited to criminal investigation and to Vida/FFG (the same email later states it correctly as 'no current criminal investigation pertaining to me'). (2) 'for want of evidence' - AGS 7 Mar 2024 only said the charges were 'withdrawn by the CDPP'; no email shows the CDPP's reason. (3) Inconsistent with Simon's 3 Apr 2025 letter to politicians (195f91451ae88f22), which says charges were 'dismissed on February 28, 2024, for want of prosecution under section 25 of the Criminal Code Act 2004'. (4) Date of ABF stop given as 25 Sep 2024 here; other accounts in the mailbox vary (25/26 Sep). (5) Confirms ABF records refer to 'law enforcement interest' - could be used by AGS to argue ongoing law-enforcement interest (i.e. cuts both ways).
- Note: Personal identifiers in the original (DOB, residential address) deliberately not reproduced in this digest.

#### 2026-04-20 14:54 UTC | REID-OUT | Re: 2023/3361 ... General Correspondence

- Summary: Letter to AGS (copied to Tribunal): the 10 April 2026 updated tender bundle proposed in the Commission's 19 March supplementary submissions has not arrived; no supplementary disclosure statement; unanswered whether a Simon-specific decision brief exists; asks if oversight or reason. Sets out six elements an individualised s 73ZN(2)(a)(iii) assessment would need. Asserts CDPP discontinued all four charges against Vida 'for no evidence' in Feb 2024 and Simon was never charged; says the decision-makers relied on the Statement of Material Facts (internal review SoR [11(f)(v)], [29(h)]). Reserves right to seek directions and invite adverse inferences.
- Quotes:
  > "The Commission's supplementary submissions proposed that an updated tender bundle would be served by 10 April 2026."
  > "The 10 April 2026 date has passed. The updated bundle has not been received. No supplementary disclosure statement has been provided. The question whether a Simon-specific decision brief exists remains unanswered."
  > "Could you please advise whether the non-delivery is the result of an administrative oversight, or whether there is a particular reason for the delay."
  > "It is Mr Reid's hope that, when produced, the documents will contain a comprehensive individualised assessment of his personal suitability, separate and distinct from the assessment of Mrs Vida Reid and of Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd."
  > "Point 6 has particular weight in light of the Commonwealth Director of Public Prosecutions' discontinuance of all four charges against Mrs Reid for no evidence in February 2024."
  > "Mr Reid himself was not charged at any stage."

'For no evidence' characterisation of the CDPP discontinuance must match the certified 23 Feb 2024 transcript. Third-person 'We write' framing by a self-represented party. Emphasising individualised assessment distinct from Vida/FFG reinforces a Simon-vs-Vida separation.

#### 2026-05-19 07:53 UTC | REID-OUT | Re: 2023/3361 ... General Correspondence (body headed: 2023/3363 — Submissions in response to Respondent's section 70 non-disclosure application dated 7 May 2026)

- Message id: `19e3f3a036032dda` | thread `19e3efe1ab6dc6e8`
- From: reid.simon4@gmail.com (Simon Reid)
- To: brisbane.registry@art.gov.au | Cc/Bcc: vida_reid@yahoo.com, Karlie.Dickinson@ags.gov.au, Craig.Land@ags.gov.au, Isabelle.Macdonald@ags.gov.au, legal@ndiscommission.gov.au
- Summary: Simon files submissions opposing the Respondent's s 70(2) ART Act application for non-disclosure orders over Attachment O to EC22-002103 and Attachment G to EC22-002521 (Respondent's letter 7 May 2026, ref 23001627). Four grounds: inconsistency waiver (Mann v Carnell), selective disclosure (Goldberg v Ng), issue waiver (DSE v Intertan), and s 70(2) discretion. Asks that the submissions go before the Deputy President before any decision.
- Quotes:
  > "At paragraph 2 of the "documents not provided" section of its 7 May 2026 letter, the Respondent expressly invited me to "make any submissions to the Tribunal you wish to make on this application by way of return correspondence." I now do so."
  > "*Mann v Carnell* (1999) 201 CLR 49 — inconsistency-based waiver. The Respondent has, on its own initiative, already disclosed substantial legal-advice content from the Commission's Legal Services Branch in the original tender bundle (including at the EC22-002000 brief references, the SL1/SL2 tracked-change comments on the draft Halton letter, and the Attachment 1D considerations document at T9.5)."
  > "*DSE (Holdings) Pty Ltd v Intertan Inc* (2003) 135 FCR 151 — issue waiver."
  > "On the present record, the public interest in the proper administration of justice, including in the fairness of these proceedings to the applicant, weighs decisively against the making of the non-disclosure orders sought."
- Attachments: Simon_Reid_Opposition_to_s70_Application_May_2026.pdf
- **EXPOSURE:** Citation accuracy (checked 26 Sep 2026 via Legal Data Hunter): the email gives Mann v Carnell as '(1999) 201 CLR 49', but the correct report is (1999) 201 CLR 1, as cited in DSE (Holdings) Pty Ltd v InterTAN Inc [2003] FCA 1191. 201 CLR 49 is the report of Esso Australia Resources v Commissioner of Taxation. DSE v InterTAN exists ([2003] FCA 1191, 30 Oct 2003); the email's report citation '135 FCR 151' could not be verified. Wrong report citations in a filed submission are a credibility risk. Sent to the Tribunal and AGS.

#### 2026-07-27 05:31 UTC | FWD | Fixed-fee limited-scope engagement — Federal Court document compliance check and lodgement (WA Registry) — clients not in WA

- Message id: `(quoted in 19fa29bffe4c6bb4)`
- From: vida_reid@yahoo.com
- To: daniel@hammondlegal.com.au (Daniel Rice, Hammond Legal)
- Summary: Privileged (seeking a retainer). Vida describes a proposed self-represented Federal Court proceeding in the WA Registry: an application under s 39B of the Judiciary Act 1903 against 'a Commonwealth statutory office holder', with two individual applicants. The documents are already prepared (Form 69, statement of claim, affidavits, Form 16 genuine steps statement, about 40-50 pages). She seeks only a compliance check, witnessing instructions for deponents outside WA (one overseas), eLodgment and service, and expects AGS Perth to accept service. Hammond Legal (Daniel Rice) replied 27 Jul 2026 07:49Z (quoted 3:49 PM AWST): it could not do eLodgment without going on the record, would help with the rest but not advise on evidentiary compliance, and would quote a fixed fee after reviewing the material. Vida forwarded this to Simon at 08:05:53Z (19fa29bffe4c6bb4). Time approximate (quoted 1:31 PM AWST).
- Quotes:
  > "I am self-represented in a proposed Federal Court proceeding in the WA Registry (an application under s 39B of the Judiciary Act 1903 against a Commonwealth statutory office holder; two individual applicants)."
  > "I am not seeking advice on merits, prospects or strategy — that is in hand."
  > "Neither applicant is currently in Western Australia"
  > "Effect service on the respondent (a Commonwealth respondent; the Australian Government Solicitor, Perth, is expected to accept service)"
  > "I'm not sure that we can attend to eLodgement as my understanding is that would generally require us to get on the record. (Hammond Legal)"
- Attachments: image001.png
- **EXPOSURE:** LOW now (privileged), but strategic. If the s 39B application is filed against the Commissioner while the three ART reviews are pending, AGS can be expected to argue it duplicates the merits review and should be refused in the Court's discretion, and to raise costs. Anything in the statement of claim and affidavits becomes available to AGS and may be compared with the ART material. This mailbox does not show whether it was filed.
- Note: Also relevant to hearing logistics: neither applicant was in WA in late July 2026.

## Appendix D - Incidental risks found outside AGS/Tribunal correspondence

- Vitality Employment Services recruiting email, 9 Feb 2023 (18634b38db658062): "simon@vitalitywa.au", signed "Director, Vitality Employment Services", recruiting disability support workers seven weeks after the banning order. This bears on compliance with the ban and on later "unemployment" claims.
- DEWR show-cause/clarification process on FFG's Workforce Australia Deed, May-Jun 2023 (188dce7e31231f36, 188e1674f618a532, 18900379e1b41879). FFG told DEWR its financial resources remained "intact" and that it had "implemented new fraud prevention strategies". DEWR noted that an org chart supplied through Tom & Co related to a different entity (Inspyre Employment Services). Simon was still trying to remove "Tori" as an FFG director at ASIC on 28 Jun 2023, which is inconsistent with the "sole director" statements.
- AFCA complaint against FFG's insurer, Mar 2023 (18701b4a9bc9cd30): "at risk of company unable to pay its creditors". This contrasts with the June 2023 DEWR statements.
- WA NDIS worker screening (2022-2024): the suspension was admitted by WA to have been made without delegation (8 Mar 2023). Simon's letter of 13 Jul 2023 (1894fefbc72ba6f2) says the banning order specifies no conduct "other than my position as director", but para 5.C of his order refers to over $1,485,527 in claims (per Vida, 189248ac1235d73e). The 15 Aug 2023 submission deadline appears to have been missed. There was a re-proposal on 11 Mar 2024, an exclusion on 8 May 2024 and an internal review application on 4 Jun 2024.
- 5 Feb 2024 email from Simon's Gmail to DMIRS: "I'm currently away FIFO", signed "Vida" (authorship ambiguous) (18d77b53e4922c44). Nov 2024: application for a permit for a light gauge steel business (1936c788f91a7f33). Both are relevant to any unemployment or hardship narrative.
- Letter to politicians, 3 Apr 2025 (195f91451ae88f22). It gives inconsistent dates and characterises the CDPP outcome as dismissal "for want of prosecution under section 25 of the Criminal Code Act 2004", a reference that looks wrong.
- Perplexity AI report generated on 27 Apr 2025 from an ART email marked OFFICIAL: Sensitive / Legal-Privilege (1967919e1a8ee702). This is a possible confidentiality issue.
- Proposed Federal Court s 39B application against "a Commonwealth statutory office holder", Jul 2026 (19fa29bffe4c6bb4, privileged). It is not known whether it was filed. If it was, AGS will likely argue duplication with the pending ART reviews.
- Vida's use of a stafflink.pro address in Jul 2026 (19fb6d623a3dad0d), with a Stafflink staff member forwarding FOI mail to her. It is ambiguous whether this reflects employment, but it could be tested against unemployment claims.

## Appendix E - Citation checks (Legal Data Hunter, 26 Sep 2026)

- "Hutchinson v AEC [2023] FCA 331" (sent to AGS and the registry, 31 Mar 2025): **wrong**. [2023] FCA 331 is *Pigozzo v Mineral Resources Ltd* (Colvin J, 3 Apr 2023).
- "NAAX v MIMIA [2003] FCAFC 64" (privileged email to Tom Monks, 13 Nov 2024): **wrong**. [2003] FCAFC 64 is *Commonwealth Bank of Australia v Duggan* (Full Court, 9 Apr 2003).
- "Mann v Carnell (1999) 201 CLR 49" (sent to the Tribunal and AGS, 19 May 2026): **wrong report citation**. *Mann v Carnell* is (1999) 201 CLR 1, as cited in *DSE (Holdings) Pty Ltd v InterTAN Inc* [2003] FCA 1191. 201 CLR 49 is the report of *Esso Australia Resources v Commissioner of Taxation*.
- "DSE (Holdings) v InterTAN (2003) 135 FCR 151" (same email): the case exists ([2003] FCA 1191, 30 Oct 2003, on legal professional privilege). The FCR report citation given **could not be verified**.
- "R v Associated Northern Collieries [1910] HCA 61", "Tomlinson v Ramsey Food Processing [2015] HCA 28" and "CQ and DIBP [2014] AICmr 91": **not resolvable** in the database used. Verify on AustLII before any reuse. Tomlinson is, to the author's knowledge, an estoppel case, not a joinder authority.
- "Rex Patrick v. Australian Government" (OAIC submission) and "section 25 Criminal Code Act 2004" (letter to politicians): **not recognisable** as cited. Treat as unverified.
